Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Depressed? No… I don’t think so, not really. It’s more that my head feels foggy, and I mix up simple things I should be able to keep straight—like appointment times, exhibit dates, or whether I already called a volunteer. New things don’t seem to stick as easily as they used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disoriented or unsure about the current year or season?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I know what year it is and what season we’re in… at least, I haven’t felt mixed up about that. If I pause, it’s usually just me thinking, not because I don’t know. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble finding the right words when you're talking or writing?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… not terribly, I don’t think, but my head feels foggy and I can lose what I’m trying to say. It’s more like I mix up simple things—appointment times, exhibit dates, or whether I already called a volunteer. I feel a little embarrassed about it, honestly. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant difficulty recalling important information or events from your past?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not important things from long ago, anyway. I can remember the big events and people in my life. I might be slow pulling something up now and then, but I haven’t noticed losing whole pieces of my past or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention to things?\n\nPatient: Yes… somewhat. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but my head has been feeling foggy, and I do lose track of simple things. Like appointment times, or dates for exhibits, or whether I already called a certain volunteer. That’s actually part of why I came in. Is that what you mean by focusing?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood or personality lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to overstate it. When the house work first started, I felt almost wonderfully charged up, like I could take on everything. But I suppose I was more sharp with people than I meant to be. I’d snap at my husband, or at the young volunteers, if they questioned my plans. It wasn’t very like me, I don’t think… or at least I hope not. Is that the kind of change you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it a big change. I’ve had a few nights where I didn’t sleep as well, but at my age that doesn’t seem so unusual. I’m still getting to bed and all that. Do you mean trouble falling asleep, or waking up, or…?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. Maybe a little, but not in a way I’d want to make too much of. I suppose I’ve been more on edge with everything going on, but I wouldn’t say I’m terribly anxious. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Depressed? No… I don’t think so, not really. I mean, everyone has a low day now and then, especially when things are unsettled, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been depressed. Is that all right—does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant changes in your physical health recently?\n\nPatient: Well… nothing major, I don’t think. I haven’t been sick exactly. I suppose my appetite hasn’t been wonderful—most days I just pick at a little toast or soup because food doesn’t sound very good. And my skirts have been a bit looser, though I wasn’t trying to lose weight. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any hallucinations?\n\nPatient: Hallucinations… no, I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed seeing or hearing things that aren’t there, at least not that I’m aware of. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It seemed to start around when we had that water damage and the remodel got going. What I’ve really noticed is more the everyday mix-ups lately—appointment times, exhibit dates, and whether I already called a certain volunteer. I mean, I might lose track of the hour if I’m busy or distracted, but I know where I am. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose it’s mostly that my head has been feeling a bit foggy lately. I keep mixing up simple things—appointment times, dates for exhibits, and even which volunteer I already called. It’s probably not terrible, but it’s been enough that I thought I should come in and ask about it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this foggy feeling in your head?\n\nPatient: Oh, I’d say… about four months now, more or less. It seemed to start around when we had that water damage and the remodel got going. I thought it was just the disruption, you know, but it’s been hanging on a bit.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering things that happened before the water damage and remodel?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s so much things from before, Doctor. What I’ve really noticed is more the everyday mix-ups lately—appointment times, exhibit dates, and whether I already called a certain volunteer. It’s embarrassing, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel unsure about where you are or what time of day it is?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so, Doctor. I mean, I might lose track of the hour if I’m busy or distracted, but I know where I am. I haven’t had that feeling of not recognizing a place or anything like that, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble learning new things recently?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more that my head feels foggy, and I mix up simple things I should be able to keep straight—like appointment times, exhibit dates, or whether I already called a volunteer. New things don’t seem to stick as easily as they used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disoriented or unsure about the current year or season?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I know what year it is and what season we’re in… at least, I haven’t felt mixed up about that. If I pause, it’s usually just me thinking, not because I don’t know. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble finding the right words when you're talking or writing?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… not terribly, I don’t think, but my head feels foggy and I can lose what I’m trying to say. It’s more like I mix up simple things—appointment times, exhibit dates, or whether I already called a volunteer. I feel a little embarrassed about it, honestly. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant difficulty recalling important information or events from your past?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not important things from long ago, anyway. I can remember the big events and people in my life. I might be slow pulling something up now and then, but I haven’t noticed losing whole pieces of my past or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention to things?\n\nPatient: Yes… somewhat. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but my head has been feeling foggy, and I do lose track of simple things. Is that what you mean by focusing?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood or personality lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to overstate it. When the house work first started, I felt almost wonderfully charged up, like I could take on everything. But I suppose I was more sharp with people than I meant to be. I’d snap at my husband, or at the young volunteers, if they questioned my plans. It wasn’t very like me, I don’t think… or at least I hope not. Is that the kind of change you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it a big change. I’ve had a few nights where I didn’t sleep as well, but at my age that doesn’t seem so unusual. I’m still getting to bed and all that. Do you mean trouble falling asleep, or waking up, or…?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. Maybe a little, but not in a way I’d want to make too much of. I suppose I’ve been more on edge with everything going on, but I wouldn’t say I’m terribly anxious. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Depressed? No… I don’t think so, not really. I mean, everyone has a low day now and then, especially when things are unsettled, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been depressed. Is that all right—does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant changes in your physical health recently?\n\nPatient: Well… nothing major, I don’t think. I haven’t been sick exactly. I suppose my appetite hasn’t been wonderful—most days I just pick at a little toast or soup because food doesn’t sound very good. And my skirts have been a bit looser, though I wasn’t trying to lose weight. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any hallucinations?\n\nPatient: Hallucinations… no, I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed seeing or hearing things that aren’t there, at least not that I’m aware of.
